# Article Build Narrative — 2026-05

Rolling log of what each scheduled-task run actually did. One entry per run, two to four sentences each. New month starts a new file.

## 2026-05-15 — Bootstrap

System initialized. Article folder created with PROJECT_PRIMER, RUNBOOK, LESSONS (seeded with 10 lessons including the Karpathy figure caveat and the recurring sentence rule), PERSONAS, and per-phase rubrics. State file initialized with `current_phase: harvest` and all 14 sections in `none` status. Scheduled task registered to fire every 2 hours. First run will be Harvest on Section 04 (Input Corpus) because it has the most pre-existing evidence in `Source Materials/`.

## 2026-05-15 — Harvest tick (Section 04)

First Harvest run executed against Section 04 (The Input Corpus). Eight evidence cards written in `manuscript/evidence/` covering top-level counts (61 .pptx, 66 .pdf), Uploaded Media substructure (135 case opinions, 12 merged packets, 81 HTML resources), the 37-file modernized-PDF subset that anchors the Prize Cases failure story, three assessment artifacts plus a midterm rubric, the email's 198-page output snapshot, the 10 cases missing source files on disk, filename heterogeneity within the flat folder, and the single-course doctrinal scope. Section 04 advanced to `evidence_status: populated`. Four pending issues logged: casebook-input status, student-work-input status, ingest-phase cost numbers, and the snapshot-date decision. New lesson L-011 added on snapshot discipline. Phase advanced to Outline; run scored 5.0 average (notes flag that non-duplication is trivially 5 on a first run).

## 2026-05-16 — Outline tick (Section 04)

First Outline run executed against Section 04. Wrote `manuscript/outlines/outline-04-input-corpus.md` with five H2 subsections (What the LMS handed us, Anatomy of the export, What the LMS did not hand us, Filename hygiene as a signal, Snapshot and disclosure) and twelve bullets total; every bullet binds to a specific evidence card or pending issue, every bullet names the inferential move that ties Section IV to its downstream payoff in Sections II, V, VII, X, or XII. Word budgets sum to exactly 900 (180 + 250 + 200 + 140 + 130). Open-questions block lists all four PI- gaps with the closing condition for each, including the snapshot recommendation that the email's 198-page figure should be the anchor pending professorial sign-off. Section 04 advanced to `outline_status: ready_for_draft`; phase rotated to Draft; lesson L-012 added on outline bullets naming the section payoff; run scored 5.0 average with a caveat that first-pass scores are inflated.

## 2026-05-16 — Draft tick (Section 04)

First Draft run executed against Section 04. Wrote `manuscript/drafts/section-04-input-corpus.md` covering all five outlined subsections (A through E) in 898 prose words against a 900-word target, with every claim tagged by a `[CITE: evidence-04-*]` placeholder pointing back to the corresponding evidence card and the PI-001, PI-002, PI-003, PI-004 gaps left as explicit `[TODO]` markers rather than papered over. Curly quotes and no em dashes were used from the start, per L-005, to reduce Polish rework. Section 04 advanced to `draft_status: needs_cite`; phase rotated to Cite; lesson L-013 added on the convention that `[CITE: ...]` placeholders should use the bare evidence-card slug so the Cite phase can resolve directly via the card's frontmatter; run scored 4.6 average against the draft rubric.

## 2026-05-16 — Cite tick (Section 04)

First Cite run executed against Section 04. Resolved all eight distinct `[CITE: evidence-04-*]` placeholders into fifteen Bluebook 21st footnotes, twelve of them forward references to `App. A (Input Corpus Inventory)` for self-descriptive corpus facts, one a forward reference to Sections III and XII for the static-website claim, one a full reporter-form list of the ten missing SCOTUS opinions (Cohens through Hamdi), and one citing the live wiki URL with a last-visited date. The 198-page snapshot was rewritten from "professor-facing progress email" to "professor-facing progress report" and routed through App. A with an internal pointer to a forthcoming `App. D (Correspondence Excerpts)` so that no personal-communication path appears in prose. WebFetch refused the wiki URL by provenance, so PI-005 logged the deferred URL-liveness check, and rubric criterion 3 took a 2; the four `[TODO]` markers for PI-001 through PI-004 remain in place because they are Harvest gaps, not Cite gaps. Section 04 advanced to `cite_status: needs_polish`; phase rotated to Polish; lesson L-014 added on the App.-A-and-D convention for case-study self-descriptive citations; run scored 3.8 average.

## 2026-05-16 — Polish tick (Section 04)

First Polish run executed against Section 04. Converted the five straight apostrophes in published prose to curly (Chandler’s, Section XII’s, Section V’s, report’s, vault’s); confirmed zero em dashes inherited from Draft per L-005. Rewrote two voice slips: replaced “Stitch will footnote the drift” with “this article footnotes the drift in case publication follows further ingestion,” removing build-system terminology from published prose, and replaced “the kind of self-consistency the thesis is selling” with “the kind of self-consistency the thesis requires,” pulling the register away from marketing. Italicized the ten case names in IV.C’s missing-cases list per Bluebook’s textual-reference rule (the prior Cite tick produced footnotes but did not italicize case names in prose, a known scope-overlap that lesson L-015 now documents). Polished file written to manuscript/polished/section-04-input-corpus.md at 897 prose words (within tolerance); Section 04 advanced to `polish_status: ready_for_stitch`; phase rotated to Stitch; run scored 4.4 average.

## 2026-05-16 — Stitch tick (partial-noop)

First Stitch run executed. Only one of fourteen sections (04) had reached `polish_status: ready_for_stitch` and no abstract exists, so the cross-section consistency criteria the Stitch rubric measures (terminology unity, fact consistency, recurring-sentence placement, transition quality, and the 10,000-to-12,000-word window) had no substrate to evaluate; the run is logged as a noop and not scored against the rubric per the dispatcher-protocol rule for empty phases. The phase still did the assembly work it could: `manuscript/full-draft.md` was created in section order with Section 04 in place and the other thirteen sections plus the abstract marked PENDING, Section 04 flipped to `polish_status: stitched` with `stitched_at` recorded, `manuscript.total_words` set to 897, `manuscript.last_stitched_at` set to 2026-05-16T11:20:00Z, and `full_draft_exists` and the new `partial_assembly` field both flipped true. The submission-readiness gate stayed false because none of its three conjuncts (word count in 10k-12k window, all sections stitched, prior clean Verify) are met. Phase advanced to Verify, where the next tick will read the full draft adversarially even though most of it is pending; lesson L-016 added on Stitch's partial-noop behavior so a future Stitch run on a similarly thin polished set knows to assemble what exists and decline to score.

## 2026-05-17 — Cite tick (Section 08 full pass plus Section 04 [^12] surgical fix)

Third-rotation Cite tick covered two sections in one phase tick: a full Cite pass on Section 08 (From Vault to Website) and a surgical fix to Section 04's footnote [^12] that closes the cite-side half of the 2026-05-16T13:20 Verify routing. Section 08 work resolved all thirteen `[CITE: evidence-08-*]` placeholders into fifteen Bluebook 21st footnotes — eleven App. A (Vault Architecture and File Layout) forward references for self-descriptive vault-infrastructure facts spanning `.site/build.py`, the hand-written SPA shell at `.site/dist/{index.html,app.js,style.css}`, `DEPLOY.md`'s Netlify CLI direct-upload procedure and three-tier fallback chain, `.site/dist/netlify.toml`'s skip-processing and SPA-redirect configuration, `RUNBOOK.md`'s Phase: Deploy section and Step 0 feedback-intake protocol, `DEPLOY.md`'s Step 3.5 four-sub-check post-deploy verification protocol (including the *Marbury* search-sanity probe), and the build script's Source Materials wikilink rendering paired with the `_headers` caching policy; one App. A plus App. D combined reference at [^1] for the URL-and-snapshot fact whose underlying record is the progress report (mirroring Section 04 [^14]'s App.-A-with-App.-D convention per L-014); three forward references to Section III for figures pending PI-019 capture per L-021 ([^2] About page, [^6] ⌘K search palette with *Marbury* query active, [^13] page-feedback form open state); and one full Bluebook citation of Sajja et al. 2023 at [^15] for the chatbot-architecture contrast Section XII develops. Bar Ass'n Standard 207 (2025) (Reasonable Accommodation for Qualified Individuals with Disabilities), the canonical ABA Standards Rules of Procedure for Approval of Law Schools provision addressing accessibility in legal education. URL `https://www.americanbar.org/groups/legal_education/accreditation/standards/standards-rules/` verified live this run via WebSearch with `allowed_domains` scoped to `americanbar.org` per L-022/L-052 (WebFetch on both the standards-rules landing page and the 2024-2025 chapter-2 PDF returned HTTP 403, the L-022 provenance-auth case; WebSearch returned the live 2025-2026 edition page reflecting all changes approved by the Section's Council and concurred in by the ABA House of Delegates through August 2025, plus a May 8, 2026 Standards Committee notice-and-comment memorandum on Standards 205 and 207, both confirming Standard 207 remains the operative disability-accommodation standard); last-visited May 30, 2026.

CRITICAL L-053 substitution: PI-248's run-316 routing note named "ABA Standard 208 (accommodations for persons with disabilities)" as the primary candidate. Primary-source verification this run showed Standard 208 is titled "Involvement of Alumni, Students and Others" and addresses how a law school may involve alumni, students, and others in a participatory or advisory capacity while the dean and faculty retain control over the educational program; Standard 208 does NOT address disability accommodation in any way. The actual disability-accommodation standard is Standard 207, "Reasonable Accommodation for Qualified Individuals with Disabilities" (formerly Standard 213 in pre-2014 editions of the Standards, renumbered in the 2014 reorganization). Trusting the routing note's "208" without verification would have anchored Section XII.E's equity-and-accessibility claim to a wholly off-point standard; the L-053 "a citation named only in a PI/Verify routing note is not verified" discipline is the controlling rule and the correct Standard 207 substituted in. PI-248's alternative suggested-fix candidate "Standard 305(d)" was also reviewed but not pursued: Standard 305 addresses "Other Academic Study" (clinical/externship/non-classroom instructional formats) and its subsection (d) does not address accessibility or accommodation in the way the XII.E equity-and-accessibility claim warrants; Standard 207 is the single on-point primary-law anchor. Per L-053 the PI-248 secondary-scholarship slot ("Dinerstein, Lamparello, or comparable canonical work") was declined: grep on `Article-Workplan.md` and `Article/research/legal-ed-scholarship.md` returned zero hits for "Dinerstein," "Lamparello," "accessibility," "disability," "accommodation," "ADA," or "Standard 208/305" as candidate sources, and no workplan-backed or independently-vetted secondary legal-education-accessibility canon source is available in the article's bibliography. Adding an unbacked secondary cite is exactly the fabrication risk L-053 prohibits; the slot stays explicitly empty and documented rather than filled with an unverified source.
